# SDK limit classification

Baseline: `2146782983`, 2026-10-28. Historical locations keep deleted limits traceable.

Categories: **1** arbitrary product cap; **2** chunk/page; **3** backpressure/credit; **4** protection or representation invariant.

Pending entries remain unfinished. A retained page must make all remaining data available through continuation. Explicit finite caller policies and the original allocation, cancellation, authorization and corruption controls remain.

## Logical total and completion owners

Source inventory composed through main `aefd8df65b`, 2026-10-29. Each row is an owner checklist entry, not a claim that the whole mechanism is complete. The constant table below retains historical file/line locations; current source and exact-source receipts govern qualification. A deleted consumer check does not close a lower-layer whole-value refusal.

For every category 1 total: default `None`, complete continuation, bounded credited residency, original authority/work/cancellation, and one final atomic publication are required. Category 2 pages/frames split work; category 3 credits delay it. Explicit finite caller capacities remain opt-in. Category 4 corruption, bomb, integer and actual OS capability checks remain; an ordinary logical value is not a frame.

| Fleet dormant state and readiness work | Each primitive's indexed pending/ready core | 3: visible/subscribed credit; no population scan | Open100k qualification: exact eventual service, bounded live RSS, first yield, zero idle timer probes/threads and ready-to-action p99. Source loop removal alone is not measured speedup. |

The scaffold `Invocation.timeout_ms = 300000` is overwritten by `ShellInput` before execution and is not a live shell deadline. Audit actual configured/preset deadlines instead; preserve every explicit user deadline and authority expiry. Deliberate fault/workload delay, actual due alarms, OS blocking waits and classified transient transport backoff are distinct from readiness polling.

Qualification uses the same accepted-byte/count/digest oracle for candidate and negative control. Reinstate the former cap, omission, prefix scheduler or polling loop and require that oracle to fail. Exercise events both before and after subscription, overflow, producer death, denied/withheld credit, never-started or abort-ignoring guests, cancellation before atomic publication, exact reload and registry digest conflicts. No missing event is repaired by a periodic timer.

## Remaining structural work

- Stream logical atomic values, command counts, paths and dynamic headers must continue across framed transports while preserving one commit and original funding.
- Actor, machine and filesystem whole-value messages still need shared streaming.
- Recursive path/JSON ownership, process event publication and remaining readiness polling need structural changes.
- Current library failures and obsolete standalone model APIs remain unresolved.

### Hosted atomic transaction policy boundary

The default whole-transaction mutation policy is absent. Proto optional presence distinguishes None from an invalid explicit zero. The service shares one count check for apply and rebase, and clients use only the advertised explicit policy. This removes no native funding, authority, cancellation, corruption or transaction conflict controls. The change adds no production allocation, I/O, round trip, task, timer or commit boundary. Existing vector staging remains amortized O(1) per mutation; a successful transaction retains one canonical atomic publication. This is an admission change, not a measured throughput claim.

The existing real authenticated TLS/gRPC control now prepares2,049 files, rebases without publication, commits once, replays the same operation, reads every name through256-entry pages, and rejects a late invalid mutation without changing the head. The existing explicitly finite service control retains client rejection and also exercises raw apply/rebase rejection with an unchanged head. Existing TypeScript HTTP encoding qualification preserves all2,049 mutations when the capability is absent and keeps the selected64-mutation negative. These controls are source-only until qualification completes. Remaining byte-envelope limits are independent unfinished work. Measure full accepted mutations/bytes, fixed-credit peak RSS, first response and total commit latency at growing counts; compare explicit finite-policy refusal and late invalid/cancelled all-or-none publication.


### Restore dependency correction (P1 review)

The first OS-lock change could fill the existing host service with waiters while its holder needed the same service to publish and unlock. BlockingWaitCredit in the shared native runtime now admits at most host-worker-count minus one potentially blocking waits. Excess callers wait in the semaphore's intrusive FIFO, not a worker or timer; none are refused. Prepare-restore acquires this credit before host submission and moves it into the original funded closure. Credit remains held through physical completion after caller cancellation. Holder finish and ordinary metadata jobs keep the ordinary service path. This introduces no second worker pool or publication mechanism.

Uncontended admission allocates no node. Contention lazily prepares both original/authority and invocation cancellation nodes on the same original ledger with their existing cleanup funding; typed admission errors remain typed. Cancellation before admission submits no native job. An accepted kernel lock wait still physically completes before its original owner retires; this does not claim prompt cancellation of an accepted flock/LockFileEx. The real native-runtime regression fills wait credit with OS-file-lock contenders and queues more than the physical worker count, checks queued cancellation/drop, then submits the holder unlock to the same host service and drains every accepted lock. No elapsed-time polling or timing assertion is used. Existing finite-original-budget, physical worker cancellation/fence and exhausted-unlock controls remain. Runtime qualification is required because this is a deadlock fix; source/compile alone is not proof.


### Registry publication reconciliation

Visibility has no SDK-owned event on either registry protocol. Publishers now perform one preflight observation and, after an actual submission, one settlement observation. npm reads the latest tag only when its exact archive digest is visible. There is no timer or readiness probe loop, and Pending is never reported as completed. Shared receipt replacement is atomic at the file-visibility boundary; it is diagnostic evidence, not an fsync/durable publication guarantee or authorization. Before submission and after its actual process return, Pending evidence is retained so a later observation failure does not erase the uncertain attempt. The original qualified source, archive digest, dependency ordering and actual registry authority remain the owners of acceptance. Explicit reconcile-only mode never calls the publication command; it revalidates qualified inputs before observing. Workflow receipt artifacts use the existing release retention policy.

Operation cost: one small JSON file write/rename before and after a publication attempt and after settlement, versus an unbounded sequence of periodic visibility reads within the old deadline. No new background task or timer. Read count is finite per explicit invocation, not a total work cap. Real npm CLI over a separate-process HTTP registry exercises absent/late-tag/exact/conflicting digests, actual source/archive receipt replacement, single observations and reconciliation without publication. Cargo uses real HTTP responses for Pending/exact/yanked/conflict projection and retains the original real-Git approved-equivalence/source-change controls. Full owning script suites and workflow contracts require qualification. No production registry throughput or propagation speedup is claimed. Measure registry request count and elapsed active/idle time per invocation, complete digest preservation and dependent-publication suppression; preserve permanent authentication, yanked version, digest conflict and uncertain native process return negatives.

The failure control uses actual Windows directory reads and a real invalid OS completion-wait source on an isolated instance of the same service. It checks no-deadline observers settling, service exit, duplicate failure suppression, future admission refusal without new native work, and unchanged pending buffer/handle/packet ownership. This code has not run on Windows. A first cross-compile caught a missing required wide-name callback in the new test sink. Corrected source `7df27303d0` passed the complete affected Windows all-target cross-compile, including the actual OS failure and retirement controls; Windows runtime remains unrun. Logical terminal failure does not prove physical I/O retirement: quarantined outstanding kernel owners stay charged and pinned. No100k-agent RSS/ready-to-action measurement or fatal-recovery throughput claim is made. Measure per-watch lifecycle cost, zero idle probes, exact fatal wait count and notification p99 at fixed original credit; preserve finite admission denial and actual OS capability errors.
